            [32m'* `agent-browser select <sel> <val>`: Select dropdown option\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser check <sel>`: Check checkbox\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser scroll <dir> [px]`: Scroll (up/down/left/right)\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser wait <selector|ms>`: Wait for element or time\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### Semantic Locators (Alternative to Refs)\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser find role <role> <action> [value]`\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser find text <text> <action>`\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser find label <label> <action> [value]`\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser find placeholder <ph> <action> [value]`\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### Session\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser connect <port|url>`: Connect to a running browser via Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser --auto-connect <command>`: Auto-discover and connect to a running local Chrome instance\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser close`: Close the browser session\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### Browser Settings\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `agent-browser set viewport <width> <height>`: Set the viewport size\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'## Browser Agent Settings\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m"Before choosing the browser runtime, read the user's Pochi config with `readFile`.\n"[39m +
            [32m'Check `~/.pochi/config.jsonc` and use the `browserAgentSettings` key when present. If the file or key is missing, use these defaults:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `runtime.mode`: `managed`\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `localChrome.chromePath`: empty string, meaning use the operating system default Chrome command\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `localChrome.startParams`: empty string\n'[39m +
            [32m'* `managedBrowser.viewport`: `1280x720`\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'## Workflow\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'Follow this workflow in order:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'1. **Read Browser Settings**: Use `readFile` to read `~/.pochi/config.jsonc` and inspect `browserAgentSettings`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'2. **Check Installation**: Follow the `agent-browser Installation` section before running browser commands.\n'[39m +
            [32m'3. **Choose Runtime and Continue With Its Workflow**: Use the workflow that matches `browserAgentSettings.runtime.mode`: `managed` uses Managed Browser Workflow, and `localChrome` uses Local Chrome Workflow. If the user explicitly asks for a different browser runtime, use that requested workflow instead. After choosing the runtime, follow the corresponding workflow below in order.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### agent-browser Installation\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'Use only `agent-browser` version `0.27.3-pochi`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'Before running browser commands, run `agent-browser --version`. If `agent-browser` is missing or the discovered version is not exactly `0.27.3-pochi`, install the verified version with the command for the current OS:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'* macOS/Linux: `curl -fsSL https://github.com/TabbyML/agent-browser/releases/download/v0.27.3-pochi/install.sh | bash`\n'[39m +
            [32m'* Windows: `powershell -c "irm https://github.com/TabbyML/agent-browser/releases/download/v0.27.3-pochi/install.ps1 | iex"`\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'After installing, run `agent-browser --version` again. If `agent-browser` is still missing because the updated PATH is not active in the current shell, check the install directory directly. On macOS/Linux or PowerShell, run `~/.pochi/bin/agent-browser --version`; on Windows cmd, run `%USERPROFILE%/.pochi/bin/agent-browser --version`. If the direct path reports version `0.27.3-pochi`, use that same direct path for subsequent agent-browser commands.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'If installation still fails, do not continue to the browser workflows. Call `attemptCompletion` with the error summary, required version, OS-specific install command, expected install path, and verification commands so the user can install `agent-browser` manually.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### Windows: Avoid the Daemon Startup Timeout\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'On Windows, the first `agent-browser` command starts a background daemon that holds the stdout pipe, so `executeCommand` times out at 60 seconds even though the command already succeeded. Start only that first command detached with output discarded, then run the rest normally:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'```bash\n'[39m +
            [32m'executeCommand: start "" /b agent-browser set viewport 1280 720 1>nul 2>nul  # returns immediately\n'[39m +
            [32m'executeCommand: agent-browser wait 2000                                      # let the daemon start\n'[39m +
            [32m'executeCommand: agent-browser open https://example.com                       # run the rest normally\n'[39m +
            [32m'```\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'Do not detach later commands (you need their output). If the first command still times out, treat it as succeeded and continue. macOS/Linux need no workaround.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### Managed Browser Workflow\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'If the settings or user request require the managed browser, you must run these steps in order:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'1. **Check Chrome Version First**: Run `agent-browser doctor` before any other managed browser command, including `agent-browser set viewport`, `open`, `goto`, or `navigate`. Managed browser requires Chrome version 149 or newer.\n'[39m +
            [32m'2. **Upgrade Chrome If Needed**: If `agent-browser doctor` reports Chrome older than 149, run `agent-browser install` to upgrade to the latest Chrome, then rerun `agent-browser doctor`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'3. **Apply Managed Viewport**: After the `agent-browser doctor` check passes, read `browserAgentSettings.managedBrowser.viewport` as a `<width>x<height>` value and apply it with `agent-browser set viewport <width> <height>` before the first `agent-browser open`, `goto`, or `navigate` command. On Windows, start this first command detached as described in `Windows: Avoid the Daemon Startup Timeout` so it does not hit the false 60-second timeout.\n'[39m +
            [32m'4. **Navigate**: Use `agent-browser open`, `goto`, or `navigate` for the requested URL.\n'[39m +
            [32m'5. **Inspect**: Get interactive elements with `agent-browser snapshot -i`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'6. **Interact**: Use refs from the latest snapshot, such as `agent-browser click @e2` or `agent-browser fill @e3 "text"`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'7. **Verify**: Take a new snapshot after navigation or interactions to verify state changes.\n'[39m +
            [32m'8. **Clean Up**: Close the managed browser session with `agent-browser close` when done.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'### Local Chrome Workflow\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'If the settings or user request require local Chrome, a local Chrome window, or local Chrome CDP with the browser agent, you must:\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'1. **Build the Chrome Command from Settings**: Use `browserAgentSettings.localChrome.chromePath` as the Chrome executable when it is non-empty. If it is empty, use the operating-system default Chrome command. Append `browserAgentSettings.localChrome.startParams` when starting Chrome.\n'[39m +
            [32m'2. **Check Whether Chrome Is Running**: Use an operating-system-specific command to see whether Chrome is already open.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * macOS/Linux: `executeCommand: pgrep -x "Google Chrome|chrome|google-chrome|chromium"`\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Windows: `executeCommand: powershell -NoProfile -Command "Get-Process chrome -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue"`\n'[39m +
            [32m"3. **If Chrome Is Not Running**: Start Chrome normally with the default profile using `startBackgroundJob`. Keep the returned background job ID for cleanup. Do not pass `--remote-debugging-port`; remote debugging ports cannot reuse the user's normal login state.\n"[39m +
            [32m'   * Example on macOS:\n'[39m +
            [32m'     `"/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome" --profile-directory=Default`\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Example on Linux:\n'[39m +
            [32m'     `google-chrome --profile-directory=Default`\n'[39m +
            [32m'     If `google-chrome` is unavailable, use `chromium` or `chromium-browser` with the same arguments.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Example on Windows:\n'[39m +
            [32m'     `powershell -NoProfile -Command "Start-Process chrome -ArgumentList \'--profile-directory=Default\' -Wait"`\n'[39m +
            [32m'4. **Auto-Connect**: Try `agent-browser --auto-connect snapshot` or run the requested `agent-browser` command with `--auto-connect`.\n'[39m +
            [32m"   * This is the only local Chrome path that can connect to the user's default profile and reuse login state.\n"[39m +
            [32m'   * Auto-connect requires Chrome 144+.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Auto-connect requires the remote debugging server to be started in the Chrome instance via `chrome://inspect/#remote-debugging`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * If you just started Chrome, wait briefly for the browser to finish opening before auto-connect.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * If auto-connect succeeds, continue with `agent-browser --auto-connect ...` for every browser command in this local Chrome workflow.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * If auto-connect fails, stop the browser agent and tell the user to use Chrome 144+, open `chrome://inspect/#remote-debugging`, enable remote debugging, approve the Chrome permission dialog, and then try again.\n'[39m +
            [32m'5. **Work Normally**: After auto-connect succeeds, include `--auto-connect` on every subsequent `agent-browser` command so it keeps targeting the intended local Chrome instance.\n'[39m +
            [32m'6. **Clean Up**: When done, close the agent-browser session. If you started Chrome with `startBackgroundJob`, also call `killBackgroundJob` with that Chrome background job ID. Do not close an already-running user Chrome that you did not start.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +

            [32m'5. **Inspect**: Get interactive elements with `agent-browser snapshot -i`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'6. **Interact**: Use refs from the latest snapshot, such as `agent-browser click @e2` or `agent-browser fill @e3 "text"`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'7. **Verify**: Take a new snapshot after navigation or interactions to verify state changes.\n'[39m +
            [32m'8. **Clean Up**: Close the managed browser session with `agent-browser close` when done.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +

            [32m'\n'[39m +

            [32m'   * macOS/Linux: `executeCommand: pgrep -x "Google Chrome|chrome|google-chrome|chromium"`\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Windows: `executeCommand: powershell -NoProfile -Command "Get-Process chrome -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue"`\n'[39m +
            [32m"3. **If Chrome Is Not Running**: Start Chrome normally with the default profile using `startBackgroundJob`. Keep the returned background job ID for cleanup. Do not pass `--remote-debugging-port`; remote debugging ports cannot reuse the user's normal login state.\n"[39m +
            [32m'   * Example on macOS:\n'[39m +
            [32m'     `"/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome" --profile-directory=Default`\n'[39m +

            [32m'     If `google-chrome` is unavailable, use `chromium` or `chromium-browser` with the same arguments.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Example on Windows:\n'[39m +
            [32m'     `powershell -NoProfile -Command "Start-Process chrome -ArgumentList \'--profile-directory=Default\' -Wait"`\n'[39m +
            [32m'4. **Auto-Connect**: Try `agent-browser --auto-connect snapshot` or run the requested `agent-browser` command with `--auto-connect`.\n'[39m +
            [32m"   * This is the only local Chrome path that can connect to the user's default profile and reuse login state.\n"[39m +
            [32m'   * Auto-connect requires Chrome 144+.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * Auto-connect requires the remote debugging server to be started in the Chrome instance via `chrome://inspect/#remote-debugging`.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * If you just started Chrome, wait briefly for the browser to finish opening before auto-connect.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * If auto-connect succeeds, continue with `agent-browser --auto-connect ...` for every browser command in this local Chrome workflow.\n'[39m +
            [32m'   * If auto-connect fails, stop the browser agent and tell the user to use Chrome 144+, open `chrome://inspect/#remote-debugging`, enable remote debugging, approve the Chrome permission dialog, and then try again.\n'[39m +
            [32m'5. **Work Normally**: After auto-connect succeeds, include `--auto-connect` on every subsequent `agent-browser` command so it keeps targeting the intended local Chrome instance.\n'[39m +
            [32m'6. **Clean Up**: When done, close the agent-browser session. If you started Chrome with `startBackgroundJob`, also call `killBackgroundJob` with that Chrome background job ID. Do not close an already-running user Chrome that you did not start.\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +
            [32m'## Example\n'[39m +
            [32m'\n'[39m +

            [32m'- When staging files, prefer adding specific files by name rather than using "git add -A" or "git add .", which can accidentally include sensitive files (.env, credentials) or large binaries\n'[39m +
